need not be in
\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
. Nonetheless, the sequence
(\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}
will still provide an approximate identity for the limit of the system.

Corollary 2.11
.

Let
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
be a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system.
Then
(\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}
forms an increasing approximate identity for
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)
.

Proof.

The sequence
(\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}
is increasing in
F_{\infty}
since the maps
\rho_{m,n}
are all c.p.c., and so by

Proposition

2.7
, it is also increasing in
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)
.

To see that this forms an approximate identity, it suffices to consider elements of the form
\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y)
for some
k\geq 0
and
x,y\in F_{k}
. Indeed, since
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra, it follows that for any
\bar{z}\in\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
, there exists
\bar{x},\bar{y}\in\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
so that
\bar{z}=\bar{x}\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\bar{y}
. Then for any lift
(x_{n})_{n},(y_{n})_{n}
of
\bar{x}
and
\bar{y}
, respectively,
Section

2

tells us
\rho_{n}(x_{n})\longrightarrow\bar{x}
and
\rho_{n}(y_{n})\longrightarrow\bar{y}
, and so
\rho_{n}(x_{n})\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{n}(y_{n})\longrightarrow\bar{x}\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\bar{y}=\bar{z}
.

Now fix
k\geq 0,x,y\in F_{k}
, and
\varepsilon>0
, and choose
M>k
so that

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\rho_{m,n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y))-\rho_{m,k}(x)\rho_{m,k}(y)\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/2\ \text{ and}

\displaystyle\|\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y)-\rho_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y))\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/2

for all
m>n>M
.
Then using
Section

2
(iii)
and (
2.7
), we have for all
n>M
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,(\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y))-\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y)\|

\displaystyle=\|e\ \big(\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,(\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y)))-\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y)\big)\|

\displaystyle=\|\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\big(\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y)\big)-\rho_{k}(x)\rho_{k}(y)\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/2+\|\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\rho_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y))-\rho_{k}(x)\rho_{k}(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.\qed

It remains to show that all
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-limits are nuclear. For this, we will use Ozawa and Sato’s one-way-CPAP, which appeared implicitly in
[
19
]
(via
[
16
]
); see
[
22
, Theorem 5.1]
for the explicit statement and its proof.

Theorem 2.12

(
[
22
,
19
]
)
.

A
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
is nuclear if and only if there exists a net
\{\varphi_{\lambda}\colon F_{\lambda}\longrightarrow A\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of c.p.c. maps from finite-dimensional
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras
\{F_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
such that the induced c.p.c. map

\displaystyle\Phi=(\varphi_{\lambda})_{\lambda}\colon\textstyle{\prod}F_{\lambda}/\textstyle{\bigoplus}F_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ell^{\infty}(\Lambda,A)/c_{0}(\Lambda,A),

given by

\Phi([(x_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}])=[(\varphi_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda}))_{\lambda\in\Lambda}],

satisfies

\iota(A^{1})\subset\Phi\big((\textstyle{\prod}F_{\lambda}/\textstyle{\bigoplus}F_{\lambda})^{1}\big),

where
\iota\colon A\longrightarrow\ell^{\infty}(\Lambda,A)/c_{0}(\Lambda,A)
denotes the identification of
A
with the sub-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra of
\ell^{\infty}(\Lambda,A)/c_{0}(\Lambda,A)
consisting of equivalence classes of constant nets.

Theorem 2.13
.

\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-limits are nuclear.

Proof.

Let
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
be a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system with complete order embedding

\Theta\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}}\colon\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)\longrightarrow F_{\infty}
from

2.7
. For each

, we define

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)
. It follows from

that each

is a c.p.c. map. We denote the sequence algebra

by
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)_{\infty}
, and we write
\iota\colon\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)\longrightarrow\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)_{\infty}

for the
embedding as equivalence classes of constant sequences.
Let
\Phi\colon F_{\infty}\longrightarrow\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)_{\infty}
be the c.p.c. map induced by the
\varphi_{m}
as in
Theorem

2.12
with

\Phi([(x_{m})_{m}])=[(\varphi_{m}(x_{m}))_{m}]
.
Note that
for

and

, we have

\displaystyle\Phi(\rho_{k}(x))

\displaystyle=\Phi([(\rho_{m,k}(x))_{m>k}])

\displaystyle=[(\varphi_{m}(\rho_{m,k}(x)))_{m>k}]

\displaystyle=[((\Theta^{-1}\circ\rho_{m})(\rho_{m,k}(x))_{m>k}]

\displaystyle=[(\Theta^{-1}\circ\rho_{k}(x))_{m>k}]

\displaystyle=\iota\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\rho_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=\iota\circ\varphi_{k}(x).

Since these elements are dense in
\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
, it follows that
\Phi(\bar{x})=\iota\circ\Theta^{-1}(\bar{x})
for each
\bar{x}\in\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\subset F_{\infty}
.
Since
\Theta^{-1}
is isometric, that gives us

\displaystyle\iota\big(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)^{1}\big)=\iota\circ\Theta^{-1}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}^{1}\big)=\Phi\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}^{1}\big)\subset\Phi(F_{\infty}^{1}).

Now with
[
22
, Theorem 5.1]
(as stated above in
Theorem

2.12
), we conclude that
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)
is nuclear.
∎

3.
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-systems from systems of c.p.c. approximations

The goal of this section is a converse to
Theorem

2.12
: Any separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra is
∗
-isomorphic to a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-limit. Moreover, the
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system will arise from a system of c.p.c. approximations. We first recall some facts regarding systems of c.p.c. approximations of separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
A
be a separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra.

(i)
A
system of c.p.c. approximations
for
A
is a sequence
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
, where the
F_{n}
are finite-dimensional
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras and
A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A
are c.p.c. maps so that for all
a\in A

\lim_{n}\|\varphi_{n}\circ\psi_{n}(a)-a\|=0.

(ii) We call a system of c.p.c. approximations as above
summable
if there exists a decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N})_{+}^{1}
such that for all
n>k\geq 0

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{n}\circ\psi_{n}\circ\varphi_{k}\|<\varepsilon_{n}.

(iii) We say a system of c.p.c. approximations as in (i) has
approximately multiplicative downwards maps
if for any
a,b\in A
, we have
\lim_{n}\|\psi_{n}(ab)-\psi_{n}(a)\psi_{n}(b)\|=0
.

(iv) We say a system of c.p.c. approximations as in (i) has
approximately order zero downwards maps
if for any
a,b\in A_{+}
with
ab=0
, we have
\lim_{n}\|\psi_{n}(a)\psi_{n}(b)\|=0
.

(v) Given a system of c.p.c. approximations as in (i), we define for all
m>n\geq 0

\displaystyle\rho_{m,n}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\psi_{m}\circ\varphi_{m-1}\circ\ldots\circ\varphi_{n}\colon F_{n}\longrightarrow F_{m}\ \text{ and }

\displaystyle\rho_{n,n}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{F_{n}}.

thus obtaining an associated system
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
.

Remarks 3.2
.

(i) We will usually consider summable systems of c.p.c. approximations, in which case
\rho_{m,n}
and
\psi_{m}\circ\varphi_{n}
become arbitrarily close for
m>n
sufficiently large. Moreover, for any
k\geq 0
and
x\in F_{k}
, the sequence
(\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)))_{n}
converges in
A
.

(ii) Asking for summability will not result in any loss of generality, since after passing to a subsystem we can always arrange this for any choice of
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N})^{1}_{+}
. Indeed, given a system
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
of c.p.c. approximations of a nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
and a decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{j})\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N})^{1}_{+}
, we can use compactness of the unit balls of the
F_{n}
to find an increasing sequence
(n_{j})_{j}\subset\mathbb{N}
so that for all
n_{k}\geq 0

and
j>k
,

\|\varphi_{n_{k}}-\varphi_{n_{j}}\circ\psi_{n_{j}}\circ\varphi_{n_{k}}\|<\varepsilon_{n_{j}}.

(iii) For a system of c.p.c. approximations of a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
as in
3.1
and any
a\in A
, we have

\lim_{n}\|a-a\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\|=0.

To see this, it suffices to consider
a\in A_{+}^{1}
. Let
\varepsilon>0
, and choose
\eta>0
so that
2\eta+\sqrt{3\eta}<\varepsilon
and
N>0
so that
\|a-\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a))\|<\eta
and
\|a^{2}-\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a^{2}))\|<\eta
for all
n>N
.
Then a consequence of Stinespring’s Theorem (
[
15
, Lemma 3.5]
), guarantees that
\|\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a)b)-\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a))\varphi_{n}(b)\|<\sqrt{3\eta}
for any
b\in(F_{n})^{1}
. Applying this to
b=1_{F_{n}}
yields

\displaystyle\|a-a\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta+\|\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a))-\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a))\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\|

\displaystyle=2\eta+\|\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a)1_{F_{n}})-\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a))\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}})\|

\displaystyle<2\eta+\sqrt{3\eta}

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

One can always ask for approximately order zero downwards maps, as follows from
[
4
]
in connection with
[
23
, Theorem 5]
); see
[
5
, Theorem 3.1]
for an explicit statement:

Theorem 3.3
.

For a separable
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
, the following are equivalent.

(i)

A
is nuclear.

(ii)

A
admits a system of c.p.c. approximations with approximately order zero downwards maps.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
be a system of c.p.c. approximations of a separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
.
Then the induced map
\Psi\colon A\longrightarrow F_{\infty}
given for all
a\in A
by

\displaystyle\Psi(a)\coloneqq[(\psi_{n}(a))_{n}],

is a complete order embedding.

If the system of approximations is summable, then we have
\Psi(A)=\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
, i.e., the image of
\Psi
agrees with the limit of the associated system in the sense of
Definitions
3.1
(v)
and
2.1
.

Proof.

Since each
\psi_{n}
is c.p.c., it follows that
\Psi
is a c.p.c. map.
Moreover, since the maps
\psi_{n},\varphi_{n}
are all c.p.c.,
it follows that

\textstyle\liminf_{n}\|\psi^{(r)}_{n}(a)\|\geq\|a\|

for all
r\geq 1
and
a\in\mathrm{M}_{r}(A)
. Hence
\|\Psi^{(r)}(a)\|\geq\|a\|
for all
r\geq 0
and
a\in\mathrm{M}_{r}(A)
,
and so
\Psi
is completely isometric and hence a complete order embedding.

Now we assume moreover that the system is summable with respect to some decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N})^{1}_{+}
.
Let
k\geq 0
and
x\in F_{k}
, and set
a\coloneqq\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x))
. (Recall that the limit exists by Remark
3
(i).)
Then we have

(3.1)

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=[(\rho_{m,k}(x))_{m>k}]

\displaystyle=[(\psi_{m}(\varphi_{m-1}(\rho_{m-1,k}(x))))_{m>k}]

\displaystyle=[(\psi_{m}(a))_{m}]

\displaystyle=\Psi(a),

and so

(3.2)

\displaystyle\textstyle{\bigcup_{n}}\rho_{n}(F_{n})=\{\Psi\big(\lim_{m}\varphi_{m}\circ\rho_{m,n}(x)\big)\mid k\geq 0,\ x\in F_{n}\}\subset\Psi(A).

On the other hand, it follows from summability
that

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{m}\circ\rho_{m,n}\circ\psi_{n}-\varphi_{n}\circ\psi_{n}\|<\textstyle{\sum}_{j=n+1}^{m}\varepsilon_{j}.

for all
m>n\geq 0
. Hence for any given
a\in A
and
\varepsilon>0
, we can find
M>0
such that for all
m>n>M
,

(3.3)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{m}\circ\rho_{m,n}\circ\psi_{n}(a)-a\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{m}\circ\rho_{m,n}\circ\psi_{n}(a)-\varphi_{n}\circ\psi_{n}(a)\|+\|\varphi_{n}\circ\psi_{n}(a)-a\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

From this, we conclude that the set
\{\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}\circ\rho_{n,k}(x)\mid k\geq 0,\ x\in F_{k}\}
is dense in
A
, and so by (
3.2
),

\textstyle{\bigcup_{n}}\rho_{n}(F_{n})

is dense in
\Psi(A)
. Since
\Psi
is isometric, that means
\Psi(A)=\overline{\textstyle{\bigcup_{n}}\rho_{n}(F_{n})}=\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
.
∎

In the situation of
Section

3
, if the system of c.p.c. approximations has approximately order zero downwards maps, then the map
\Psi
clearly is order zero. In this case, since the codomain is
F_{\infty}
, we obtain refined information about the positive contraction appearing in the structure theorem for order zero maps (
Theorem

1.4
).

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
be a system of c.p.c. approximations of a separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
with approximately order zero downwards maps, and let
\Psi\colon A\longrightarrow F_{\infty}
be the order zero complete order embedding from
Section

3
.
We write
C
for the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra generated by
\Psi(A)
in
F_{\infty}
and
J_{C}=\{b\in F_{\infty}\mid bC\cup Cb\subset C\}
for the idealizer of
C
in
F_{\infty}
, and we denote by
h
the element in
\mathcal{M}(C)
provided by the structure theorem for order zero maps; cf.
Theorem
1.4
.

Then there exists
\bar{h}\in(J_{C})_{+}^{1}\subset F_{\infty}
so that
\bar{h}c=hc

for all
c\in C
. In particular, for all
a,b\in A
,

(3.4)

\displaystyle\bar{h}\Psi(a)

\displaystyle=\Psi(a)\bar{h},

(3.5)

\displaystyle\|\bar{h}\Psi(a)\|

\displaystyle=\|\Psi(a)\|=\|a\|,\ \text{ and }

(3.6)

\displaystyle\bar{h}\Psi(ab)

\displaystyle=\Psi(a)\Psi(b).

Moreover, (
3.4
), (
3.5
), and (
3.6
) still holds when replacing
\bar{h}
by any
\bar{e}\in(F_{\infty})_{+}^{1}
such that
\bar{e}\Psi(a)=\bar{h}\Psi(a)
for all
a\in A
.

Proof.

Since
C
is an ideal in
J_{C}
, there is a unique
∗
-homorphism
\theta\colon J_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}(C)
extending the canonical embedding
C\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}(C)
so that
\theta(b)c=bc
and
c\theta(b)=cb
for all
b\in J_{C}
and
c\in C
. We claim that
\theta
is surjective.
Indeed, let
\pi\colon\prod F_{n}\longrightarrow F_{\infty}
denote the quotient map. Since
C
is separable, we can find a separable sub-
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
D\subset\prod F_{n}
with
\pi(D)=C
. Though
D
may be degenerate in
\prod F_{n}
(in the sense that its support projection is not
1_{\prod F_{n}}
), it is non-degenerate in its ultra-weak closure
\overline{D}^{\sigma\text{-wk}}\subset\prod F_{n}
(in the same sense), and so
[
1
, Proposition 2.4]
tells us we can
identify
\mathcal{M}(D)
with
\{b\in\overline{D}^{\sigma\text{-wk}}\mid bD\cup Db\subset D\}\subset\prod F_{n}
,
and with this identification, we have
\pi(\mathcal{M}(D))\subset J_{C}
.
Since
D
is separable and
\pi|_{D}\colon D\longrightarrow C
is a surjection,

[
1
, Theorem 4.2]
guarantees a surjective
∗
-homomorphism
\hat{\pi}\colon\mathcal{M}(D)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}(C)
so that for all
d\in D
and
x\in\mathcal{M}(D)
we have

\hat{\pi}(x)\pi(d)=\pi(xd)
and
\pi(d)\hat{\pi}(x)=\pi(dx)
. All this together gives us the following diagram, which we claim commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}(D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}(C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi|_{D}}

\subseteq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi|_{\mathcal{M}(D)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\pi}}

\subseteq

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

To see that
\hat{\pi}=\theta\circ\pi|_{\mathcal{M}(D)}
, let
x\in\mathcal{M}(D)
and
c\in C
, and choose
d\in D
such that
\pi(d)=c
. Then

\displaystyle\theta(\pi(x))c=\pi(x)c=\pi(x)\pi(d)=\pi(xd)=\hat{\pi}(x)\pi(d)=\hat{\pi}(x)c,

and likewise
c\theta(\pi(c))=c\hat{\pi}(x)
.
Hence
\hat{\pi}=\theta\circ\pi|_{\mathcal{M}(D)}
, and
\theta
is surjective as desired.

Now let
\bar{h}\in(J_{C})_{+}^{1}
be a lift of
h
.
Then we have for each
c\in C

\displaystyle\bar{h}c=\theta(\bar{h})c=hc\hskip 14.22636pt\text{ and }\hskip 14.22636ptc\bar{h}=c\theta(\bar{h})=ch.

It then follows from our choice of
h

that for all
a,b\in A
,

\displaystyle\bar{h}\Psi(ab)

\displaystyle=h\Psi(ab)=\Psi(a)\Psi(b),\ \text{ and }

\displaystyle\bar{h}\Psi(a)

\displaystyle=h\Psi(a)=\Psi(a)h=\Psi(a)\bar{h}.

Since
\Psi
is isometric,
\|a\|^{2}h=\|\Psi(aa^{*})\|h\geq\Psi(aa^{*})
for all
a\in A
(see Remark
1
(i)), and so

\displaystyle\|a\|^{4}

\displaystyle=\|\Psi(a)\|^{4}

\displaystyle=\|\Psi(a)^{*}\Psi(a)\Psi(a)^{*}\Psi(a)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Psi(a)^{*}\Psi(aa^{*})\Psi(a)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Psi(a)^{*}\|a\|^{2}h\Psi(a)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|^{3}\|h\Psi(a)\|.

In particular, for all
a\in A

\displaystyle\|\bar{h}\Psi(a)\|=\|h\Psi(a)\|=\|\Psi(a)\|=\|a\|.

Finally, suppose
\bar{e}\in(F_{\infty})_{+}^{1}
and
\bar{e}\Psi(a)=\bar{h}\Psi(a)=h\Psi(a)
for all
a\in A
. Then for each
a\in A
,

\|\bar{e}\Psi(a)-\Psi(a)\bar{e}\|=\|h\Psi(a)-\Psi(a)\bar{e}\|=\|h\Psi(a)^{*}-\bar{e}\Psi(a)^{*}\|=0,

and it follows that
\Psi(a)\bar{e}=\Psi(a)\bar{h}=\Psi(a)h
for all
a\in A
as well.
Since this extends to all
c\in C
, it follows that
\bar{e}\in(J_{C})_{+}^{1}
and
\theta(\bar{e})=h
. Since
\bar{e}
is also a lift of
h
, the preceding arguments establish (
3.4
), (
3.5
), and (
3.6
) for
\bar{e}
.
∎

Now we are ready to prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 3.6
.

Let
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
be a system of c.p.c. approximations of a separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
with approximately order zero downwards maps. After possibly passing to a subsystem of approximations, the associated system
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
is a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system in the sense of
Definition

2.2
, and the map
\Psi\colon A\longrightarrow F_{\infty}
from
Section

3
is an order zero complete order embedding with
\Psi(A)=\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
.

Upon composing
\Psi
with the inverse of
\Theta
from
Proposition
2.7
, we obtain an isomorphism
\Theta^{-1}\circ\Psi\colon A\longrightarrow\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,}\big(\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}\big)
. In particular, any separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra is
∗
-isomorphic to a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-limit.

Proof.

As we saw in Remark
3
(ii), after possibly passing to a subsystem, we may assume that the system of approximations (and any further subsystem) is summable.

Let
\Psi\colon A\longrightarrow F_{\infty}
be the induced order zero complete order embedding from
Section

3
,
h
be the element in
\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{C}^{*}(\Psi(A)))
from the structure theorem for order zero maps (
Theorem

1.4
), and
\bar{h}\in(F_{\infty})_{+}^{1}
be an element guaranteed by
Proposition

3.5
so that
\bar{h}\Psi(a)=h\Psi(a)
for all
a\in A
.
We fix a lift
(h_{n})_{n}\in(\prod F_{n})_{+}^{1}
of
\bar{h}
and note that since

\displaystyle\|\Psi(b)\Psi(a)-\bar{h}\Psi(a)\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: h3}}{=}\|\bar{h}\Psi(ba)-\bar{h}\Psi(a)\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: h3}}}{=}\|\bar{h}\Psi(ba-a)\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: h2}}{=}\|ba-a\|

for all
a,b\in A
,
we have that for any
a,b\in A
and
\varepsilon>0
there exists
M>0
so that for all
m\geq M

(3.7)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{m}(b)\psi_{m}(a)-h_{m}\psi_{m}(a)\|<\|ba-a\|+\varepsilon.

Now we are ready to determine an appropriate subsystem of approximations. Fix a countable dense subset
\{a_{k}\}_{k}\subset A^{1}
and a strictly decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N})_{+}^{1}
.
By Remark
3
(iii) we may choose
j_{0}>0
so that

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{j_{0}}(1_{F_{j_{0}}})a_{0}-a_{0}\|<\varepsilon_{0}/2.

Then we can choose
j_{1}>j_{0}
so that for all
m\geq j_{1}
and
i\in\{1,2\}

\displaystyle\|\psi_{m}(\varphi_{j_{0}}(1_{F_{j_{0}}}))\psi_{m}(a_{0})-h_{m}\psi_{m}(a_{0})\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: approx 1}}{<}\|\varphi_{j_{0}}(1_{F_{j_{0}}})a_{0}-a_{0}\|+\varepsilon_{0}/2

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: approx 1}}}{<}\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq k\leq 1}\|\varphi_{j_{1}}(1_{F_{j_{1}}})a_{k}-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: approx 1}}}{<}\varepsilon_{1}/2,\text{ and}

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{j_{1}}(\psi_{j_{1}}(\varphi_{j_{0}}(1_{F_{j_{0}}})^{i}))-\varphi_{j_{0}}(1_{F_{j_{0}}})^{i}\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: approx 1}}}{<}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}/3.

For all
n>0
we may inductively choose
j_{n}>j_{n-1}
so that for all
m\geq j_{n}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
,

(3.8)

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq k<n}\|\psi_{m}(\varphi_{j_{n-1}}(1_{F_{j_{n-1}}}))\psi_{m}(a_{k})-h_{m}\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n-1},

(3.9)

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq k\leq n}\|\varphi_{j_{n}}(1_{F_{j_{n}}})a_{k}-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n}/2,\text{ and}

(3.10)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{j_{n}}(\psi_{j_{n}}(\varphi_{j_{n-1}}(1_{F_{j_{n-1}}})^{i}))-\varphi_{j_{n-1}}(1_{F_{j_{n-1}}})^{i}\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n}^{2}/3.

Let
\hat{\Psi}\colon A\longrightarrow\prod F_{j_{n}}/\textstyle{\bigoplus}F_{j_{n}}
denote the map induced by the subsystem
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{j_{n}}}F_{j_{n}}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{j_{n}}}A)_{n}
.
Then
\hat{\Psi}
is still an order zero complete order embedding, and
for
\hat{\bar{h}}=[(h_{j_{n}})_{n}]\in\prod F_{j_{n}}/\textstyle{\bigoplus}F_{j_{n}}
we still have
\hat{\bar{h}}\hat{\Psi}(a)=\hat{h}\hat{\Psi}(a)
for all
a\in A
, where
\hat{h}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{C}^{*}(\hat{\Psi(A)}))
is the element guaranteed by
Theorem

1.4
.
Thus we may pass to the (summable) subsystem
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{j_{n}}}F_{j_{n}}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{j_{n}}}A)_{n}
, drop the subscripts, and write
\Psi
,
h
, and
\bar{h}
for
\hat{\Psi}
,
\hat{h}
, and
\hat{\bar{h}}
, respectively.
Now (
3.8
)–(
3.10
) say that for any
n>0
, we have for for
m>n
and
i\in\{1,2\}

(3.11)

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq k\leq n}\|\psi_{m}(\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}}))\psi_{m}(a_{k})-h_{m}\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n},

(3.12)

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq k\leq n}\|\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}})a_{k}-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n}/2,\text{ and}

(3.13)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(\varphi_{n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})^{i}))-\varphi_{n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})^{i}\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n}^{2}/3.

We aim to show that the associated system
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
as in Definition
3.1
(v) is
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
.

Claim 1:

For all
a\in A
, we have
[(\rho_{n+1,n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}]\Psi(a)=\bar{h}\Psi(a)
, and (
3.4
), (
3.5
), and (
3.6
) also hold for
[(\rho_{n+1,n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}]
in place of
\bar{h}
.

We check the claim on an element
a_{k}
from our fixed dense subset of
A^{1}
. Since

(3.14)

\displaystyle\|\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})-h_{n}\psi_{n}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: second approx1}}}{=}\|\psi_{n}(\varphi_{n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}}))\psi_{n}(a_{k})-h_{n}\psi_{n}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: second approx1}}{<}\varepsilon_{n-1}

for all
n>k
, it follows that
[(\rho_{n+1,n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}]\Psi(a)=\bar{h}\Psi(a)
for all
a\in A
, which establishes the claim by
Proposition

3.5
with
\bar{e}=[(\rho_{n+1,n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}]
.

Claim 2:
For any
a\in A
and
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an
M>0
so that for
m>n>M

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\psi_{m}(a)-\rho_{m,m-1}(1_{F_{m-1}})\psi_{m}(a)\|<\varepsilon.

Again, we check the claim on some
a_{k}
from our fixed dense subset of
A^{1}
. Fix
\varepsilon>0
. It follows from summability
(see Remark
3
(i)) that we may choose
M>k
with
\varepsilon_{M}<\varepsilon/3
so that
\|\rho_{m,n}-\psi_{m}\circ\varphi_{n}\|<\varepsilon/3
for all
m>n>M
.
Then it follows from (
3.11
) and (
3.14
) that for all
m>n>M
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\psi_{m}(a_{k})-\rho_{m,m-1}(1_{F_{m-1}})\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle<\|\psi_{m}(\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}}))\psi_{m}(a_{k})-\rho_{m,m-1}(1_{F_{m-1}})\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|+\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle\leq\|\psi_{m}(\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}}))\psi_{m}(a_{k})-h_{m}\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\|h_{m}\psi_{m}(a_{k})-\rho_{m,m-1}(1_{F_{m-1}})\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|+\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n}+\varepsilon_{m-1}+\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

Claim 3:
For any
a,b\in A
and
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an
M>0
so that
m>n>M

\displaystyle\|\psi_{m}(a)\psi_{m}(b)-\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\psi_{m}(ab)\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

Let
a,b\in A^{1}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Claim 1 tells us that
[(\rho_{n+1,n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}]\Psi(ab)=\Psi(a)\Psi(b)
, and so there is an
M_{0}>0
so that for all
m>M_{0}

\displaystyle\|\psi_{m}(a)\psi_{m}(b)-\rho_{m,m-1}(1_{F_{m-1}})\psi_{m}(ab)\|<\varepsilon/2.

Then this with Claim 2 (for
ab
in place of
a\in A
and
\varepsilon/2
in place of
\varepsilon
) tells us that there exists
M>M_{0}
so that for all
m>n>M
,

\displaystyle\|\psi_{m}(a)\psi_{m}(b)-\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\psi_{m}(ab)\|

\displaystyle<\|\rho_{m,m-1}(1_{F_{m-1}})\psi_{m}(ab)-\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\psi_{m}(ab)\|+\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

Claim 4:

For any
a\in A
and
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an
M>0
so that for all
m>n>M
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a)\big)-\psi_{m}(a)\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

Again, we check the claim on some
a_{k}
from our fixed dense subset of
A^{1}
. Fix
1>\varepsilon>0
.
By (
3.13
) and
[
14
, Lemma 7.11]
, for each
n>0

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})\big)-\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}}))\ \varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a_{k}))\|

\displaystyle<\big(3\varepsilon_{n}^{2}/3\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{n}.

We now can choose
M>k
with
\varepsilon_{M}<\varepsilon/6
so that for all
m>n>M
,

(3.15)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})\big)-\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}}))\ \varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a_{k}))\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{n}

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/6,

(3.16)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a_{k}))-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/6,

and

(3.17)

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}(\psi_{n}(a_{k}))-\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{m-1}\circ\rho_{m-1,n}(\psi_{n}(a_{k}))-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/2.

Then we have for all
m>n>M
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})\big)-\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}}{\leq}\|\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})\big)-\rho_{m,n+1}(\psi_{n+1}(a_{k}))\|

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\overset{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}{\leq}{}}+\|\rho_{m,n+1}(\psi_{n+1}(a_{k}))-\psi_{m}(a_{k})\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: claim 4.3}}{<}\varepsilon/2+\|\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})\big)-\rho_{m,n+1}(\psi_{n+1}(a_{k}))\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}}{=}\varepsilon/2+\|\rho_{m,n+1}\circ\psi_{n+1}\big(\varphi_{n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})\big)-a_{k}\big)\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}}{\leq}\varepsilon/2+\|\varphi_{n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(a_{k})\big)-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: claim 4.1}}{<}\varepsilon/2+\varepsilon/6+\|\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}}))\ \varphi_{n}(\psi_{n}(a_{k}))-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: claim 4.2}}{<}2\varepsilon/3+\varepsilon/6+\|\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}}))a_{k}-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}}{\leq}5\varepsilon/6+\|\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}}))a_{k}-\varphi_{n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})a_{k}\|

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\overset{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}{\leq}{}}+\|\varphi_{n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})a_{k}-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}{<}5\varepsilon/6+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}/3+\|\varphi_{n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})a_{k}-a_{k}\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: second approx2}}{<}5\varepsilon/6+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}/3+\varepsilon_{n-1}/2

\displaystyle\overset{\phantom{\eqref{eq: second approx3}}}{<}\varepsilon.

This finishes the proof of the claim.

Now we are ready to verify that
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
is a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system.
Let
k\geq 0
,
x,y\in F^{1}_{k}
, and
\varepsilon>0
. Define
a\coloneqq\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x))\in A
and
b\coloneqq\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(y))\in A
. (Recall that these limits exist by Remark
3
(i).) We then in particular have

\displaystyle\lim_{n}\|\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)-\psi_{n}(a)\psi_{n}(b)\|=0.

Combining this with Claims 3 and 4, we can choose
M>0
so that for all
m>n\geq M
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,k}(x)\rho_{m,k}(y)-\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\psi_{m}(ab)\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/4\ \text{ and}

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(ab)\big)-\psi_{m}(ab)\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon/2.

Using
these two approximations, we compute for
m>n,l>M
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,l}(1_{F_{l}})\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)\big)-\rho_{m,k}(x)\rho_{m,k}(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho_{m,l}(1_{F_{l}})\ \rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)-\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(ab)\big)\|

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\|\rho_{m,l}(1_{F_{l}})\ \rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(ab)\big)-\rho_{m,l}(1_{F_{l}})\ \psi_{m}(ab)\|

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\|\rho_{m,l}(1_{F_{l}})\psi_{m}(ab)-\rho_{m,k}(x)\rho_{m,k}(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)-\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(ab)\|

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\|\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,n-1}(1_{F_{n-1}})\psi_{n}(ab)\big)-\psi_{m}(ab)\|

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\|\rho_{m,l}(1_{F_{l}})\psi_{m}(ab)-\rho_{m,k}(x)\rho_{m,k}(y)\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

Finally, since our subsystem is still summable,
\Psi(A)=\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(F_{n},\rho_{n})}_{n}
by
Section

3
, so by
Section

2

A
is
∗
-isomorphic to a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-limit.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

If
A
is a separable unital nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra with a summable system
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
of c.p.c. approximations with approximately order zero downwards maps, then
(\varphi_{n}(1_{F_{n}}))_{n}
converges to
1_{A}
, and hence
\Psi(1_{A})=\lim_{n}\rho_{n}(1_{F_{n}})=e
. In this case, the associated system
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
will automatically be a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system even without passing to a subsystem.

We close this section with a description of the product of a nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra in terms of an associated
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system, interpreting
Section

2
along the lines of
Proposition

2.6
.

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
be a summable system of c.p.c. approximations of a separable nuclear
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
with approximately order zero downwards maps so that the associated system
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
is a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system. Let
\Psi\colon A\longrightarrow F_{\infty}
be the induced order zero complete order embedding. Then for any
k\geq 0
,
x,y\in F_{k}
, and
a=\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x))
,
b=\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(y))\in A
, we have

\Psi(ab)=\Psi(a)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\Psi(b)=\lim_{n}\rho_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y))
.

Proof.

Like in (
3.1
) we have
\Psi(a)=\rho_{k}(x)
and
\Psi(b)=\rho_{k}(y)
, and so by Propositions
2.6
and
2.7
,

\displaystyle\Psi(ab)

\displaystyle=\Theta^{-1}(\Psi(ab))

\displaystyle=\big(\Theta^{-1}\circ\Psi(a)\big)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\big(\Theta^{-1}\circ\Psi(b)\big)

\displaystyle=\rho_{k}(x)\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-2.5)\circle*{3.0}\end{picture}\,\,\,\rho_{k}(y)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n}\rho_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)).\qed

4.
NF systems and
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-systems

We conclude by comparing our
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-systems with Blackadar and Kirchberg’s NF systems from
[
4
]
and establish that the latter may indeed be regarded as a special case of the former.

Definition 4.1

(
[
4
, Definitions 2.1.1 and 5.2.1]
)
.

A system
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
consisting of finite dimensional
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras and c.p.c. maps as in Definition
2.1
is called an
NF system
if the maps
\rho_{m,n}
are
asymptotically multiplicative
, in the sense that for all
k\geq 0
,
x,y\in F_{k}
, and
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
M>k
so that for all
m>n>M
,

\|\rho_{m,n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y))-\rho_{m,k}(x)\rho_{m,k}(y)\|<\varepsilon.

The limit of this system (in the sense of
Definition

2.1
) is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra with multiplication given for all
k\geq 0
and
x,y\in F_{k}
by

\rho_{k}(x)\rho_{k}(y)=\lim_{n}\rho_{n}(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)).

A
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra that is
∗
-isomorphic to the inductive limit of an NF system is called an
NF algebra
.

Remark 4.2
.

A unital
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system is NF, and if
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
is an NF system such that for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
M>0
so that for all
m>n>M
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})-1_{F_{m}}\|<\varepsilon,

then it is also a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system. With
Theorem

4.4
below, one can drop this condition upon passing to a subsystem.

The following excerpt from
[
4
, Theorem 5.2.2]
characterizes nuclear and quasidiagonal
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras in terms of NF systems. In comparison with
Theorem

3.6
, here one asks for approximately multiplicative downwards maps as opposed to just approximately order zero ones; this corresponds to adding quasidiagonality to the nuclearity hypothesis.

Theorem 4.3
.

For a separable
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
A
the following are equivalent:

(i)

A
is an NF algebra.

(ii)

A
is nuclear and quasidiagonal.

(iii)

There exists a system
(A\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}F_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}A)_{n}
of c.p.c. approximations with approximately multiplicative downwards maps.

Theorem 4.4
.

Any NF system admits a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-subsystem.

Proof.

Let
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
be an NF system. From this we form another NF system
(B_{n},\varphi_{n+1,n})
(with a different limit) just as in
[
4
, Proposition 5.1.3]

by taking
B_{n}=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{n}F_{j}
and
\varphi_{n+1,n}\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{B_{n}}\oplus\rho_{n+1,n}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}

=

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1,0}}

=

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2,1}}

=

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{1,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

\bigoplus

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

\bigoplus

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{2,1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

\bigoplus

Then for each
k\geq 0
,
\hat{x}=(x_{0},...,x_{k})\in B_{k}
, and
n>k
, we have

\varphi_{n,k}(\hat{x})=\hat{x}\oplus\oplus_{j=k+1}^{n}\rho_{j,k}(x_{k}).

We claim we can find a subsystem
(B_{n_{j}},\varphi_{n_{j}+1,n_{j}})_{j}
which is
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
. Set
B\coloneqq{\overset{\hbox to28.85pt{\vbox to2.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.65276pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -39.37 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.2pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 L -0.55 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{(B_{n},\varphi_{n})}_{n}}
. Then each
\varphi_{n+1,n}
is a complete order embedding, and hence so are the induced c.p.c. maps
\varphi_{n}\colon B_{n}\longrightarrow B\subset B_{\infty}
.
Using Arveson’s Extension Theorem (cf.
[
4
, Lemma 5.1.2]
), we extend each c.p.c. contraction
\varphi_{n}^{-1}\colon\varphi_{n}(B_{n})\longrightarrow B_{n}
to a c.p.c. map
\psi_{n}\colon B\longrightarrow B_{n}
with
\psi_{n}\circ\varphi_{n}=\mathrm{id}_{B_{n}}
.
Since the union
\bigcup_{n}\varphi_{n}(B_{n})
is nested,

(\varphi_{m}\circ\psi_{m})|_{\varphi_{n}(B_{n})}=\mathrm{id}_{\varphi_{n}(B_{n})}
for all
m>n\geq 0
, and so
(B\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}B_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}B)_{n}
is a system of c.p.c. approximations of
B
. Note that the associated system
(B_{n},\psi_{n+1}\circ\varphi_{n})_{n}
is exactly
(B_{n},\varphi_{n+1,n})_{n}
: Indeed, for any
n\geq 0
and
x\in B_{n}
, we have for all
m>n

(4.1)

\displaystyle(\psi_{m}\circ\varphi_{m-1}\circ\psi_{m-1}\circ\ldots\varphi_{n+1}\circ\psi_{n+1})(\varphi_{n}(x))

\displaystyle=\psi_{m}(\varphi_{n}(x))

\displaystyle=\psi_{m}(\varphi_{m}(\varphi_{m,n}(x)))

\displaystyle=\varphi_{m,n}(x).

Moreover, the downwards maps of our system
(B\xlongrightarrow{\psi_{n}}B_{n}\xlongrightarrow{\varphi_{n}}B)_{n}
of c.p.c. approximations are approximately multiplicative. To see this, it suffices to consider
\varphi_{k}(x),\varphi_{k}(y)
for some
k\geq 0
and
x,y\in B_{k}
. Fix
\varepsilon>0
. Since
\bigcup_{n}\varphi_{n}(B_{n})
is dense in
B
, we may choose
N>k
and
z\in B_{N}
so that
\|\varphi_{N}(z)-\varphi_{k}(x)\varphi_{k}(y)\|<\varepsilon/3
. Since the system is NF, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{k}(x)\varphi_{k}(y)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}\big(\varphi_{n,k}(x)\varphi_{n,k}(y)\big)\overset{\eqref{eq: jump}}{=}\lim_{n}\varphi_{n}\big(\psi_{n}(\varphi_{k}(x))\psi_{n}(\varphi_{k}(y))\big),

and so we may choose
M>N
so that

\|\varphi_{k}(x)\varphi_{k}(y)-\varphi_{m}\big(\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(x))\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(y))\big)\|<\varepsilon/3

for all
m>M
.
Since each
\varphi_{n}
is isometric, we have for all
m>M>N

\displaystyle\|\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(x)\varphi_{k}(y))-\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(x))\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(y))\|

\displaystyle<\|\psi_{m}(\varphi_{N}(z))-\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(x))\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(y))\|+\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle=\|\varphi_{m}\big(\psi_{m}(\varphi_{N}(z))\big)-\varphi_{m}\big(\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(x))\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(y)))\big)\|+\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle=\|\varphi_{N}(z)-\varphi_{m}\big(\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(x))\psi_{m}(\varphi_{k}(y))\big)\|+\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

Since the downwards maps are approximately multiplicative, they are also approximately order zero, and so
Theorem

3.6
guarantees that the associated system
(B_{n},\psi_{n+1}\circ\varphi_{n})_{n}=(B_{n},\varphi_{n+1,n})_{n}
, has a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-subsystem
(B_{n_{j}},\varphi_{n_{j+1},n_{j}})_{j}
.

We claim that the system
(F_{n_{j}},\rho_{n_{j+1},n_{j}})_{j}
is also
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
. Since this will hold without passing to a further subsystem, at this point we may drop the subscripts for ease of notation.
Fix
k\geq 0
,
x,y\in F_{k}
, and
\varepsilon>0
. Let
\hat{x}=(0,...,0,x),\hat{y}=(0,...,0,y)\in B_{k}
, and choose
M>k
so that for all
m>n,l>M
we have

(4.2)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{m,l}(1_{B_{l}})\varphi_{m,n}\big(\varphi_{n,k}(\hat{x})\varphi_{n,k}(\hat{y})\big)-\varphi_{m,k}(\hat{x})\varphi_{m,k}(\hat{y})\|

\displaystyle<\varepsilon.

Expanding these terms, we get

\displaystyle\varphi_{m,k}(\hat{x})\varphi_{m,k}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=\big(\hat{x}\oplus\big(\oplus_{j=k+1}^{m}\rho_{j,k}(x)\big)\big)\big(\hat{y}\oplus\big(\oplus_{j=k+1}^{m}\rho_{j,k}(y)\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\hat{x}\hat{y}\oplus\big(\oplus_{j=k+1}^{m}\rho_{j,k}(x)\rho_{j,k}(y)\big),

\displaystyle\varphi_{m,l}(1_{B_{l}})

\displaystyle=1_{B_{l}}\oplus\big(\oplus_{j=l+1}^{m}\rho_{j,l}(1_{F_{l}})\big),

and

\displaystyle\varphi_{m,n}(\varphi_{n,k}(\hat{x})\varphi_{n,k}(\hat{y}))

\displaystyle=\varphi_{m,n}\big(\hat{x}\hat{y}\oplus\big(\oplus_{j=k+1}^{n}\rho_{j,k}(x)\rho_{j,k}(y)\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\hat{x}\hat{y}\oplus\big(\oplus_{j=k+1}^{n}\rho_{j,k}(x)\rho_{j,k}(y)\big)\oplus\big(\oplus_{j=n+1}^{m}\rho_{j,k}\big(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)\big)\big).

By considering only the
m^{\text{th}}
entries of these, it follows from (
4.2
) that

\displaystyle\|\rho_{m,n}(1_{F_{n}})\rho_{m,n}\big(\rho_{n,k}(x)\rho_{n,k}(y)\big)-\rho_{m,k}(x)\rho_{m,k}(y)\|<\varepsilon,

which shows that
(F_{n},\rho_{n+1,n})_{n}
is a
\mathrm{CPC}^{*}
-system.
∎
